Valid legacy notes and all historical records remain readable. This is a source-derived negative reproduction, **not** a reported customer incident, direct browser trial or empirical cup result.

**SAFE change in SAME COMMIT:** `site/lib/engine.js` filters historical prefills with the pre-existing `measuredRecipeShots(shots)` bounds (dose 5–40 g, yield 5–200 g, time 1–180s) before dose, grind and temperature eligibility. ### Exact source → current behavior → priority

**R455-SAFE-001 — Preserve verified gates (no change).** `site/lib/constants.js` defines exactly five required 1–5 taste ratings and exact all-green ideal `{extraction:3,body:3,clarity:5,sweetness:5,astringency:1}`. `site/lib/engine.js` lines 406–412 abstain on incomplete scores, preserve successful green shots regardless of numeric time and supply nonnumeric preparation diagnostics on high astringency / low clarity. `tests/recommendation-research-guardrails.test.mjs` already includes corresponding cases, as does `tests/next-shot-control-consistency.test.mjs`. These source behaviors align with owner Locked five-scale/all-green/one-change decisions and the qualified report #01/#08 framework. **Status: existing behavior inspected; no code modification or newly executed tests**.

**R455-DEC-001 — Low-sweetness-only automatic yield change; OWNER PRODUCT DECISION REQUIRED before altering behavior.** `site/lib/engine.js` lines 406–431: for completed scores `{extraction:3,body:3,clarity:5,sweetness:4,astringency:1}`, `isDialed` is false, the evenness/extraction/body/temperature branches do not apply, and the default `A small refinement` branch generates a yield `+1 g` (unless the 200 g limit blocks). No source condition in that branch establishes that sweetness alone implies longer yield. Report #01 says sensory descriptors are downstream of multiple causes; #08 explicitly warns against treating extraction/time or model mechanisms as demonstrated sensory benefit. This is a **deterministic static control-path observation**, not an executed app test or proof that the current heuristic tastes worse. Owner choice: **(A)** keep cautious +1 g exploratory adjustment, clearly label as exploratory, or **(B, recommended pending review)** offer a nonnumeric taste/repeat diagnostic when sweetness is the only failing metric, keeping recorded dose/yield/grind unchanged. This changes user-facing recommendation policy and must **not** be implemented from research alone. Proposed acceptance: unit fixture tests with all-green, sweetness-only, low clarity, extreme yield bound and different styles; browser/mobile user wording if approved. Priority P2 / visible recommendation trust.

**R455-TECH-001 — Local edit suggestion consistency; needs runtime/ownership confirmation before safe fix.** `site/lib/engine.js` `editShot` lines 462–471 updates scored taste fields but then overwrites `old.suggestion` with a generic `Shot corrected` object (with `next:null`) when not all-green. `recommendation` lines 355–383 prefers a cached suggestion over regenerating advice on that latest shot. In the **local mutable engine path** this can leave corrected taste ratings without a corresponding next adjustment. However, `site/cloud/mapping.js` lines 59–66 **recomputes** the latest shot suggestion on hydration, and `applyPending` only queues local `save_shot`; live Cloud edit behavior may therefore differ. **Status: code-path inconsistency observed, user-facing Cloud defect UNVERIFIED; not changed.** Priority P2 pending narrow offline/online edit trajectory reproduction and exact-source regression; avoid writes to server/old shot/history or applying a current setup's grinder limits to a historical edited shot. Existing related checks: `tests/next-shot-control-consistency.test.mjs`, `tests/cloud-shot-history-actions.test.mjs`, `tests/cloud-draft-recommendation-context.test.mjs`. #26 owns focus/accessibility, #96 owns Auth/privacy; coordinate before overlapping modifications.

- [Barista Hustle Espresso Compass](https://www.baristahustle.com/the-espresso-compass/) explicitly describes fixed-dose increased yield = weaker and more extracted, while less even extraction compresses the sweet zone and extreme fining is not monotonically helpful. [Barista Hustle Dose](https://www.baristahustle.com/lesson/b1-2-01-dose/) describes basket capacity and dose confounds. **Professional practical sources, not a blind controlled taste trial**. - [World Coffee Research Sensory Lexicon 2.0](https://worldcoffeeresearch.org/resources/sensory-lexicon) confirms 110 attributes and formal **Sour, Bitter, Salty** references (2017). It does not establish that `hollow`, `muted` or finish duration uniquely diagnose a grinder/puck fault. A sensory descriptor is not a chemical extraction measurement.
- [Hendon, Colonna-Dashwood & Colonna-Dashwood 2014 ACS full original](https://pubs.acs.org/doi/full/10.1021/jf501687c) applies density-functional theory to binding of seven model coffee components and Na/Ca/Mg; does NOT supply an experimentally proven universal espresso mineral prescription or sensory-benefit rule. Their conclusion also notes no one water composition works for all coffees.
- Prior #455 [two-path model check](https://arxiv.org/abs/2206.12373) is reused as model-level plausibility, not recertified as observed channeling in a given user shot. Bitter+sour+weak may suggest unevenness; cannot infer verified channels or taste benefit from ratings alone.

**Original/author-source checks with limits:** [Gagné 2023 source analysis](https://coffeeadastra.com/2023/09/21/what-i-learned-from-analyzing-300-particle-size-distributions-for-24-espresso-grinders/) personally analyzes ~300 PSDs/24 grinders (same coffee and Camsizer) and finds overlapping conical/flat categories, no universal RPM/burr-size association; observational, no randomized architecture or blinded taste result. [Vaca Guerra et al. 2023](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jfoodeng.2022.111301), Hamburg university record of original Journal of Food Engineering study, finds poorer PSD uniformity can produce lower packed-bed porosity at the same axial compression even with larger mean particle sizes; does not imply every grinder/coffee combination. [Wadsworth et al. 2026](https://doi.org/10.1098/rsos.252031), original 2026 Royal Society Open Science paper with university-deposited final text, uses two coffees and 11 grinder settings plus XCT/lattice Boltzmann model, supports connected pore/surface/permeability relation; not proof of ideal flavor settings. [Uman et al. 2016 full original](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC4834475/) shows bean temperature effects on the tested grinder PSD and cannot justify universal calibration offsets. (2026), *Royal Society Open Science*, 13:252031, DOI [10.1098/rsos.252031](https://doi.org/10.1098/rsos.252031), [final published institutional PDF](https://strathprints.strath.ac.uk/95930/1/Wadsworth-etal-RSOS-2026-A-model-for-the-permeability-of-coffee-pucks-validated.pdf), first-party [university archive](https://strathprints.strath.ac.uk/95930/). Two coffees, 11 grind settings per coffee (22 packs), XCT pore network and digital intrinsic-permeability simulations; air analogue viscosity 1.8205 ×10^-5 Pa·s, density 1.2047 kg/m3, tiny prescribed pressure gradient and very small Reynolds number (~1.36×10^-7 to 2.51×10^-6). This is low-Re numerical flow in measured structures, **not a set of 22 verified espresso cup trials or sensory improvements**. Authors explicitly do not simulate the actual inertial regime in §5.3, and extrapolate using prior porous-media relationships. Their ~38–42g/27–32s/92°C case is an illustrative model scenario, **not universal espresso target**. Conclusions identify unmeasured evolving saturation, fines, CO2 and pressure impacts. Direct publisher PDF bytes could not be downloaded/h​​ashed, so source SHA-256 remains NULL; visual/text reading verifies only the cited passages, NOT independently replicating their numerical findings.

Three in-house coffee sources in light/dark roast forms were tested on a Mahlkönig EK43. Water added *before grinding* at 20–30 μL/g reduced measured surface charging 50–60% in tested samples; a specific dark-Yogondoy grinder-retention condition changed about 12% untreated to 2.5% with 10 μL/g. On dark Temascaltepec, the water intervention changed flow/time and increased TDS/concentration; tested lighter roast did not show comparable significant brew changes. Chute ionization suppressed charge but did **not** reproduce full-grinder retained-fines/espresso-flow effects. These are specific physical/chemical endpoints, **not** blinded sensory preference, a validated sweetness target, universal RDT amount, or equivalence to grinder slow-feed. The original article additionally identifies possible caking/corrosion/bacterial risks at excess moisture. Publisher article excerpts were accessible via indexed original full text; direct NIH PMC HTML open returned reCAPTCHA and publisher PDF/HTML original bytes were not downloaded/hash-verified. This is a bounded original claim audit, **not complete primary paper byte certification**. Separate 2024 Matter and 2023 arXiv reports remain distinct.

**Reproduced deterministic defect (P2 UX/correctness):** Existing model Git blob `f48dc8967e2c2f7038c9324fdabca4348f5cb12d` compares nonblank `workspace.recent` and `createdAt` dates as raw ISO lexical strings in `visibleCoffees(...,'recent')`, and also compares them in `hideCoffee` replacement selection. Counterexample: `2026-11-05T03:00:00+02:00` represents 01:00 UTC, older than `2026-11-04T23:30:00-07:00` (06:30 UTC), yet lexical order says otherwise. Invalid restored string `z-not-a-date` can win over both. A JavaScript comparator reproduction yielded pre-fix `invalid, olderUtc, newerUtc` vs actual-instant `newerUtc, olderUtc, invalid`. This is a derived-list/latest-selection issue, **not** corruption or loss of original coffee/shot data.

**Narrow SAFE fix:** Replace the local `sortableHistoryDate` text key with a pure `sortableHistoryInstant` that returns finite `Date.parse` instant, else `-Infinity`; use one reverse-chronology comparator for both `visibleCoffees` primary recent / createdAt fallback and `hideCoffee` eligible next current coffee. Two file paths changed: `site/lib/model.js` (candidate Git blob `4ee51bb3f430b424d92d54590eb3c5706d0b80f8`) and `tests/issue455-coffee-browse-legacy-dates.test.mjs` (candidate blob `e4d0912d261d34a8af4feb505c29ec911c1b354c`). ## November 5, 2026 UTC — R455-045 slow-feed primary source unit and conditional PSD verification [skip netlify]

**Source-first receipt:** Development parent `db887cdaaa3baa4bbdf6b72596bedf43abb4ca80` (main `c25e31343b1974b89445b48b43993bc446aa96ca`, release/production `b386a977a7c43bf190f6315105894334d96f0f18`). AGENTS, entrypoint/preflight, Master Specification/Decision Ledger, approval matrix, CURRENT_STATUS, release queue, source register, #455/#335/#267/#33/#96 and concurrent #267/#33/#504 work recovered. Repository reports PUBLIC under separate owner-tracked #504 exception; no visibility change. GitHub #267 strict V2 frozen/current literal line credit **zero** here.

**Directly verified new primary evidence:** Gagné's 2019 original author article [Grind Quality and the Popcorning Effect](https://coffeeadastra.com/2019/04/12/grind-quality-and-the-popcorning-effect/) (same light Guji/10g, Baratza Forté BG, hopper/full/bean-by-bean) gives **0.08 mm² average particle surface-area difference** for one-bean-at-a-time vs full hopper, not **0.08 mm** diameter. [Kaffeemacher's own 2025 slow-feeding article](https://kaffeemacher.de/en/blogs/kaffeewissen/slow-feeding) incorrectly restates this as linear 0.08 mm. Original research report #06 archived SHA256 `e36835579fadf24cd2fdb7738d597b93f8bd23908c52206e48ccfe9e69904a80`, current Git blob `9128527d3208379c723d561eee6ffbe09d174f55`, source lines 321–322, 462–463 directly crosschecked. **Critical study boundary:** Four laser-measured grind particle distributions of a medium-dark Arabica blend, 3 initial fixed-chamber densities (360/400/480 kg/m3, 7.2/8.0/9.5g), three independent replicate beds per condition. Rig corrected for showerhead/filter pressure loss, 10Hz ultrasonic flow. Water at 80C was circulated for **600s to fully extract the beds**, THEN pressure/flow steps sampled for **60s per step** (last 30s averaged); the paper did *not* use 600s normal espresso shots. Original Table2 shows permeability K **2.59×10^-14 to 4.38×10^-13 m²** (~17× range). Adding 2.3g density in fixed-volume chamber decreased permeability 3–4× across tested conditions. This is engineering steady-state material behavior, not normal unsteady espresso flavor or user 5-sensory-score evidence. Careful qualifier for historical 600s language: report #06 says 600s pre-extracted, which is correct; newly distinguished downstream 60s measurement steps, not a correction to the original text.

**Authority and live source:** development parent `206697361ed621b0ef8f862b3a9ab9026bcbcf25`; main `b62bc028fe1cda1fd2756bfb1162501ca629002a`, release/production `6c729b928fad5fd59ae012c63d7c005f180b2b64`. Repo reported PUBLIC under separately tracked #504 exception, no visibility write.

**Publisher-original evidence and limitations:** [Andueza, Vila, de Peña & Cid (2007), *Influence of coffee/water ratio on the final quality of espresso coffee*, Wiley, JSFA 87(4) 586–592, DOI 10.1002/jsfa.2720](https://scijournals.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/abs/10.1002/jsfa.2720), version-of-record January 15 2007. Direct first-party **ABSTRACT ONLY**, not full article methods, raw PDF, participant-level sensory scores or verified beverage density. Authors compared coffee/nominal liquid-water preparation by botanical coffee and roast, proposed **6.5 g per 40 mL and 7.5 g per 40 mL** in some conventional roast conditions, 6.5 g/40 mL for torrefacto. Torrefacto adds sugar DURING ROAST, and its descriptors included bitterness/astringency/burnt/acrid/earthy: these do not validate DIC perceived sweetness as measured sucrose. Crucially **mL beverage volume is NOT measured g beverage mass**, so no universal transfer to the app's doseG/yieldG mass ratio or a one-variable dose/recommendation step. Publisher abstract alone does not verify 8.5 g full experimental treatment, exact instrument, panel n or statistics. Agric. Food Chem. 51(24):7034–7039, DOI 10.1021/jf034628f](https://pubs.acs.org/doi/10.1021/jf034628f) ORIGINAL American Chemical Society publisher abstract, independently matched to [PubMed PMID 14611167](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/14611167/). Online 18 October 2003, in issue 19 November 2003. **Only first-party abstract and publisher/PubMed metadata reviewed, NOT full six-page paywalled paper**; raw PDF bytes SHA256, complete methods, grinder physical burr/PSD, exact dose/recipe, panel size/randomization/statistical tables/individual measurements NOT verified. No purchase made.

**Two distinct claims bounded:** Within the stated 20% Arabica/80% Robusta blend, natural vs a condition with 50% of Robusta torrefacto roasted (sugar added during roast), the study compared coarse, fine, very-fine classes. In these conditions, its authors selected **fine** for natural but **coarse** for torrefacto. More finely ground material correlated with higher solubles/aroma, yet the study did NOT prove all coffees want finer; its selected grind endpoint varied by processing. Authors attribute part of greater foam/extraction in torrefacto to caramelized sugar/melanoidins, and **suggest** (do not experimentally establish for all coffees) an acceptable EY up to **25%** for this studied variant. EY is not a directly measured user's five taste ratings; chemical sugar does not equal perceived sweetness. Do not copy universal grind/EY/taste targets into app or confuse origin/processing with calibration.

Directly reviewed full publisher HTML methods (three trials), results and conclusions, affiliations and conflict declaration. Trial 1: five batches of same commercial coffee, 15 shots per mode per batch = 150, 15g dose and 92°C/9bar, with three 5g layers coarse-bottom/standard-middle/fine-top versus uniform grinder setting; trial 2: five brands / 150 extractions; trial 3: additional coffee blends. Authors state no human subjects; none of the five DIC taste sliders, slow-feed compensation or Turbo/Fast-Flow superiority was measured. Exact original article PDF/raw flow series not accessed/hashed or independently reanalyzed; HTML review only.
